> AGENT

Both done. Summary:

## Two-cents on the code (8/10)

**On-spec MVP, no scope creep.** The ticket sprint produced exactly what the brief asked for:

- ✅ Schema migration with `tickets`, `ticket_epics`, `codebases.root_commit_sha` + indexes
- ✅ `megaplan/tickets/` module (4 files, ~1000 LOC): identity, file frontmatter, core ops, mode-aware dispatch
- ✅ All 9 CLI verbs registered; `ticket new` prints only the ULID; `--json` works
- ✅ Auto-address hook wired into **both** `DBStore.record_epic_event` and `FileStore.record_epic_event` (same surface, no parallel path)
- ✅ Planner prompt assembly includes open-tickets section ranked by tag overlap then recency
- ✅ Agent instructions updated
- ✅ **Offline queue / `.pending/` correctly deferred** (no traces in code) — biggest scope-discipline win
- ✅ 50/50 non-DB tests pass; 8 DB tests skip cleanly without `SUPABASE_DB_URL`
- ✅ Smoke test against a scratch repo: ULID-only stdout, correct frontmatter, status transitions work

**Drift (minor):**
- CLI accepts `--tags` only; brief specified `-t` short form
- `final.md` task reporting lists every changed file under every task (reporting bug, not a code bug)
- Cloud-mode tests are gated on `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, so without a live DB they're effectively unverified

**Notable judgment call (good):** Migration deliberately drops the `bot_turns(id)` FK on `filed_in_turn_id` to accommodate synthetic `plan_worker_{plan_id}` markers — divergence from the design doc, documented inline with a "don't add this back without addressing synthetic markers" warning. Exactly the kind of call premium reasoning is for.

## Megaplan meta-feedback (filed at `.megaplan/plans/tickets-mvp/feedback.md`)

Stage ratings: plan 7, critique 8, revise 7, gate 7, finalize 9, execute 8, review 7. Total $12.85 / ~57 min for a tier-3 standard run with 4 critique-revise cycles.

**Main learnings:**
- 4 critique iterations was driven by initial plan having factual errors about code locations — `--with-prep` would likely have fixed in one pass for integration-dense work like this
- Revise cycles absorbed one issue at a time instead of batching — `depth=medium` on revise specifically might be worth testing
- Review didn't catch the `-t` flag drift — worth checking whether the review prompt explicitly compares CLI surface to the brief

Nothing breaks. The MVP is mergeable as-is once you cast eyes on it.

> DEVELOPER

which profile did you use there? to execute

> AGENT

`thoughtful` — tier 3, at default `standard` robustness and `low` depth. So in shorthand: `thoughtful` (no modifiers). Single-vendor Claude on the premium phases (plan/critique/revise/review), DeepSeek V4 Pro on the mechanical phases (prep/gate/finalize/execute).

> DEVELOPER

did you run all the migrations?

> AGENT

No — I didn't run any migrations. The smoke test was local-only mode (scratch git repo, no DB). The DB path is unverified:

- `supabase/migrations/202605112100_tickets.sql` was created on disk but never applied
- 8 cloud-mode tests skipped because `SUPABASE_DB_URL` isn't set in this shell
- So the SQL itself hasn't been syntax-checked against Postgres, let alone the CRUD helpers in `store/db.py`

Want me to apply it? Two options depending on where you usually run migrations:

1. **Local Supabase** — `supabase db reset` or `supabase migration up` if you have the CLI + local stack running
2. **Remote / hosted** — point `SUPABASE_DB_URL` at the dev instance and run the migration + cloud tests against it

Which environment do you want me to target?

> AGENT

Mixed. The good and the bad:

**Good:** Vertical, per-feature layout (`tickets/`, `editorial/`, `resident/`, `review/`, `execute/`, `loop/`) — easy to find a feature; new modules slot in cleanly (the tickets MVP did exactly that).

**Bad — three structural smells:**
1. **44 top-level subdirs under `megaplan/`** — that's sprawl. Hard to tell at a glance which are core, which are features, which are utilities. A tier (`core/`, `features/`, `infra/`) would help.
2. **Mega-files:** `store/db.py` (4,134 LOC), `store/file.py` (3,011), `workers.py` (2,491), `cli.py` (2,180), `auto.py` (1,696). `db.py` especially is a future-grief magnet — already saw it growing again for tickets. Worth splitting into per-domain modules (epics, plans, codebases, tickets, feedback, …).
3. **Naming friction:** `_core/` vs `cloud/` vs root-level `chain.py`/`workers.py`/`auto.py`/`hermes_worker.py` — unclear which layer owns what. A reader has to grep to know.

It's not unhealthy — it's a codebase that grew fast and hasn't been compressed in a while. A "structural audit" sprint (split db.py, group subdirs, document the layering) would pay back quickly. The good news: the per-feature pattern means doing it incrementally is safe.

> AGENT

Here's how I'd approach it. The codebase isn't broken — it's just sprawled. Three independent cleanup axes, sorted by ROI:

## Axis 1 — Split the mega-files (highest ROI, lowest risk)

The two single biggest grief sources:

- **`store/db.py` (4,134 LOC)** — split by domain into `store/db/epics.py`, `plans.py`, `codebases.py`, `tickets.py`, `feedback.py`, etc., with `db.py` becoming a thin facade that re-exports. Same for `store/file.py` (3,011 LOC). The CRUD per table is already mostly contiguous in the file, so the split is mechanical, not architectural.
- **`cli.py` (2,180 LOC), `workers.py` (2,491), `auto.py` (1,696)** — same pattern: each has internal sections that want to be sibling files.

Risk: low — no behavior change, just file moves with re-exports for compatibility. Done as a series of small PRs, each independently mergeable.

## Axis 2 — Consolidate the 23 root-level `.py` files

There are real organization smells here:

- **Duplication of concept:** `audit.py` (file) + `audits/` (dir), `feedback.py` (file) + `tickets/` (dir treating same kind of thing differently), `hermes_worker.py` + `workers.py`, `execution_timeout.py` floating alongside `execute/`.
- **No layer signal:** `cli.py`, `flags.py`, `types.py`, `key_pool.py`, `control.py`, `codex_pricing.py` are infra; `chain.py`, `auto.py`, `workers.py` are workflow; `evaluation.py`, `verifiability.py`, `progress.py`, `iteration_pressure.py` are quality/observability. A reader can't tell.

Target shape (illustrative, not prescriptive):
```
megaplan/
  _core/        cross-cutting primitives (already exists, expand)
  workflow/     auto, chain, workers, hermes_worker, phase_result
  store/        with db/ and file/ sub-packages
  features/     tickets/, feedback/
  quality/      audit*, evaluation, verifiability, progress, iteration_pressure
  infra/        cli, flags, types, control, key_pool, codex_pricing
  ... (existing: cloud, resident, editorial, profiles, prompts, ...)
```

Risk: medium — every `from megaplan.X` import in the codebase changes. But mechanical (`grep -r` + sed) and verifiable (tests still pass).

## Axis 3 — Name and document the layering

44 top-level entries is unnavigable not because there's *too much*, but because nothing labels what's a feature vs. infra vs. workflow vs. legacy. Even without moving files, a `megaplan/ARCHITECTURE.md` that classifies each module would cut the cognitive load by half. Often this is what you actually want before moving anything — it surfaces the real groupings instead of guessing.

---

**Suggested sequence:**

1. **Architecture audit doc** (½ day) — classify every module. Cheap, defensible. Reveals which moves matter and which are noise.
2. **Split `store/db.py`** (1 sprint, `thoughtful//low`) — single biggest win, well-contained.
3. **Split `cli.py` and `workers.py`** (1 sprint each, `basic` or `led`) — mechanical.
4. **Reorganize roots into layers** (1 sprint, `thoughtful` with prep) — only after the audit clarifies which file goes where.
5. **Optional later:** flatten the smaller subdirs that aren't pulling weight.

**Main tradeoff to know:** every move breaks `from megaplan.X import Y` somewhere. You'll trade one churn (the moves) against the future grief of growing files. Worth it for `db.py`; possibly not worth it for files under 1,000 LOC unless they're conceptually misplaced.

Want me to start with the architecture audit doc, or jump straight to splitting `db.py`?

```
1	---
2	name: megaplan
3	description: AI agent harness for coordinating Claude and GPT to make and execute extremely robust plans.
4	---
5	
6	# Megaplan
7	Route every step through the `megaplan` CLI. Never call agents directly.
8	Before the first CLI call, resolve a working launcher and reuse it for the whole run. Do not assume `megaplan` itself is on `PATH`; command presence alone is not enough. Prove the launcher works by successfully running a harmless CLI call with it first. In the instructions below, treat `<launcher>` as that verified command.
9	Launcher resolution order:
10	1. Try `python -m megaplan config show`.
11	2. If that fails, try `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`.
12	3. If that fails, try `uv run python -m megaplan config show`.
13	4. If that fails, try a version-selected shim such as `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan config show`.
14	5. Only use bare `megaplan ...` if that exact form already succeeded during this check.
15	## Triage
16	A single megaplan can cover as much as 2 weeks of work — don't reflexively split large efforts into multiple plans. Pick the right level based on the task:
17	- **Skip megaplan**: single-file fixes, bug fixes with clear cause, simple refactors, config changes, adding tests for existing code. Just do it.
18	- **Light**: multi-file changes with clear scope, well-understood features, straightforward additions. One critique pass, no gate, no review.
19	- **Standard** (default for megaplan): cross-cutting changes touching many subsystems, unfamiliar codebase areas, ambiguous requirements, changes with high breakage risk, or anything where the plan itself needs debate.
20	- **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4.
21	
22	Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan.
23	## Modes
24	Megaplan has two output modes, picked with `--mode` at `init`:
25	- **`--mode code`** (default): the run produces a code diff. Execute workers emit per-task file changes. Use for features, refactors, bug fixes, migrations — anything whose deliverable is source code.
26	- **`--mode metaplan`** (alias: `--mode doc`): the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <relative/path>` (e.g. `docs/design.md`). The prep, execute, and review phases use authoring-specific prompts; the execute schema uses `sections_written` instead of file changes; auditing reasons about section delivery. Use for design docs, architecture specs, research notes, RFCs, proposals, post-mortems, migration plans — anything whose deliverable is prose, not code. This is the "design-first / preplan" workflow; `prep` is the visible repository-investigation *phase* inside every run (both modes have it), not a separate mode.
27	
28	`--from-doc <relative/path>` works with either mode. The path must be relative to `--project-dir`, must stay inside that directory, and must point to an existing file. When present, `init` imports any `## Settled Decisions` section from that prior doc artifact and stores the source path for later planning and execution context.
29	
30	All other flags (`--robustness`, `--auto-approve`, `--phase-model`, `--hermes`, subagent mode, overrides, step editing) behave identically in both modes. The workflow phases are the same: `prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review`.
31	
32	A common pattern is two runs: first `--mode metaplan` to produce a rigorous design document, then `--mode code --from-doc docs/design.md` on a new idea that references that document to implement it.
33	
34	**`--mode` and `--output` go together.** `init` rejects `--output` without `--mode metaplan` (error `invalid_args`), and rejects `--mode metaplan` without `--output`. Don't try to pass one without the other.
35	## Start
36	Run `<launcher> config show` before `init`. If `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly present, do not ask the execution-mode question and honor that configured override, including configured `false`. If that raw key is absent, ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) before `init`. In the same config check, respect `execution.robustness` as a settable override when it is configured; otherwise pick robustness yourself per the triage guidance above.
37	```bash
38	<launcher> init --project-dir "$PROJECT_DIR" [--auto-approve] [--robustness light|standard|robust|superrobust] [--mode code|metaplan] [--output docs/foo.md] [--from-doc docs/prior.md] "$IDEA"
39	```
40	For metaplan-mode runs, pass `--mode metaplan --output <relative/path>` (the path is where the final document artifact is written, relative to the project dir). Everything else is identical to code mode.
41	Pass `--from-doc <relative/path>` when the new run should inherit decisions from a prior doc artifact. The path must be relative to the project dir, must exist as a file, and can be used with either `--mode code` or `--mode metaplan`. When the source doc contains a `## Settled Decisions` section, megaplan imports those decisions and automatically promotes them into success criteria for the new plan: `load_bearing: true` decisions become `must` criteria and `load_bearing: false` decisions become `info` criteria.
42	## Settled Decisions Section Format
43	When authoring a doc artifact that makes design decisions, use either of these canonical markdown shapes (the parser accepts both):
44	
45	Bold-dash inline shape (preferred for short decisions):
46	```md
47	## Settled Decisions
48	
49	- **SD-001** \u2014 Keep the current storage model. _load_bearing: true_
50	  Rationale: External integrations depend on it.
51	- **SD-002** \u2014 Default model is claude-sonnet-4-6. _load_bearing: false_
52	  Rationale: Balance of speed and capability.
53	```
54	
55	YAML-ish shape (preferred when decisions need more fields):
56	```md
57	## Settled Decisions
58	- id: SD-001
59	  load_bearing: true
60	  decision: Keep the current storage model
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/data/tickets_skill.md",
  "content": "# Megaplan tickets\n\nTickets are short, repo-scoped notes on problems, bugs, or \"we should look at this\" observations against a codebase. They live as committed `.md` files under `<repo>/.megaplan/tickets/` and (when a cloud store is configured) mirror to `tickets` / `ticket_epics` tables. Tickets are inert until folded into an epic — they capture problems, not work.\n\n## When to file a ticket\n\nReach for `megaplan ticket new` when:\n- During epic or plan execution you notice an **out-of-scope problem** that should be tracked but isn't blocking the current task.\n- The user explicitly asks you to **capture an observation** (\"file a ticket for that\", \"make a note of this for later\").\n- You want to log a rough edge or potential bug whose fix would be its own piece of work.\n\nDo **not** use tickets for:\n- Guidance/corrections to *how the tooling itself behaves* — that's `megaplan feedback` (a separate, plan-scoped concept).\n- In-flight task state — that's the executor's own checklist.\n- General notes or chat — write them in the conversation.\n\n## Filing\n\n```bash\nmegaplan ticket new \"<title>\" -b \"<body>\" [--tags tag1,tag2]\n```\n\nConventions:\n- `-b \"...\"`, `--edit` (open `$EDITOR`), and `-` (read body from stdin) are mutually exclusive; one is required.\n- `ticket new` prints **only the ULID** on stdout on success (logs go to stderr). Capture it for piping.\n- `--tags` is comma-separated. Tags are freeform; common ones: `bug`, `refactor`, `tech-debt`, `observability`, `docs`, `cross-repo`.\n- `source` is auto-derived: if `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is set (the agent is running under a megaplan-launched worker), `source=agent` and the turn id is recorded; otherwise `source=human`.\n- The repo is resolved from cwd via its root commit SHA (`git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`). Identity survives rename / transfer / remote change.\n\nMulti-line body via stdin:\n```bash\ncat <<'EOF' | megaplan ticket new \"Title here\" --tags tag1,tag2 -\nFirst paragraph of the body.\n\nMore detail in a second paragraph.\nEOF\n```\n\n## Reading\n\n```bash\nmegaplan ticket list   [--status open|addressed|dismissed] [--tags t1,t2] [--json]\nmegaplan ticket show   <id> [--json]\n```\n\n`--json` on read commands emits structured output; safe for piping.\n\n## Editing and linking\n\n```bash\nmegaplan ticket edit    <id> [--title ...] [--body ...] [--status ...] [--add-tag ...] [--remove-tag ...]\nmegaplan ticket link    <ticket> <epic> [--resolves]\nmegaplan ticket unlink  <ticket> <epic>\nmegaplan ticket addressed <id> [--note \"...\"]\nmegaplan ticket dismiss   <id> [--reason \"...\"]\nmegaplan ticket reopen    <id>\n```\n\n`link ... --resolves` marks the join with `resolves_on_complete=true`. When the linked epic transitions to `done`, the ticket auto-flips to `addressed` and gets a `resolution_note` referencing the epic. Idempotent — re-running on an already-addressed ticket is a no-op.\n\n## Modes\n\nMegaplan runs in one of two modes; tickets work transparently in both:\n\n- **Local-only** (no cloud store configured) — `.md` files on disk are the sole source of truth. `codebase_id` will be `null` in frontmatter; identity is computed on demand from the repo's root SHA.\n- **Cloud-configured** (`SUPABASE_DB_URL` or megaplan config points at a store) — every operation writes the `.md` file **and** mirrors to the `tickets` / `ticket_epics` tables. Auto-registers the codebase on first ticket if needed.\n\nYou do not need to detect the mode yourself; the CLI dispatches based on whether a store is configured.\n\n## Local format\n\n```\n<repo>/.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md\n```\n\n```markdown\n---\nid: 01HXY...\ntitle: Execute step retries swallow stderr on timeout\ntags: [execute, observability]\nstatus: open\nsource: agent\ncodebase_id: null            # null in local-only, populated in cloud\ncreated_at: 2026-05-11T14:22:00Z\nepics:\n  - id: epic_01HZ...\n    resolves_on_complete: true\n---\n\n# Body\n\nMarkdown prose describing the issue.\n```\n\nCommit ticket files. The `.md` is the human-readable artifact and (in local-only) the system of record.\n\n## Discovery at planning time\n\nWhen a new epic is created or refined for the current repo, the planner automatically surfaces open tickets ranked by tag overlap with the epic's goal and recency. The planner may propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. You don't need to remind it — discovery is built into the plan-phase prompt assembly.\n\n## Quick reference\n\n| You want to … | Run |\n|---|---|\n| File a ticket | `megaplan ticket new \"title\" -b \"body\" [--tags t1,t2]` |\n| Pipe a multi-line body | `cat body.md \\| megaplan ticket new \"title\" -` |\n| List open tickets | `megaplan ticket list --status open --json` |\n| Link a ticket to an epic so it auto-closes | `megaplan ticket link <tid> <eid> --resolves` |\n| Mark addressed manually | `megaplan ticket addressed <tid> --note \"...\"` |\n| Reopen a closed ticket | `megaplan ticket reopen <tid>` |\n\nThat's the whole surface. The auto-address hook does the rest.\n"
}

name: megaplan-tickets
description: File and manage megaplan tickets — short, repo-scoped notes on problems or observations that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.

# Megaplan tickets

Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on problems, bugs, or "we should look at this" observations against a codebase. They live as committed `.md` files under `<repo>/.megaplan/tickets/` and (when a cloud store is configured) mirror to `tickets` / `ticket_epics` tables. Tickets are inert until folded into an epic — they capture problems, not work.

name: megaplan-rubric
description: Pick the right megaplan profile, thinking-strength tier, and robustness level for the work in front of you — for both Codex and Claude harnesses. Consult before invoking megaplan.
---

# Megaplan rubric — three questions, three dials

Every piece of work answered with three questions. Each question points at one dial. The dials are **independent** — work through each one ignoring the others — then **weigh the three together** holistically to land on a coherent judgment.

| | Question | Dial | What it scales | Flag |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What level of raw capability does this need? | **Intelligence tier** | `$/call` | `--profile` |
| 2 | What level of process rigor does this need? | **Planning complexity** | `# of calls` | `--robustness` |
| 3 | How deeply does each model need to think? | **Depth** | `tokens/call` | `--depth` (with `--phase-model` as the surgical escape hatch) |

The dials aren't a mechanical lookup; they're three lenses you apply to the same task, and the answers should fit each other. A high tier with low robustness is usually a mismatch; so is a low tier with `max` depth. When the three feel like they're pulling in opposite directions, the work probably needs to be split.

**The dials measure residual complexity, not nominal scope.** Two factors shape residual complexity, pointing in opposite directions:

- **Decisions already made decrease it.** A brief where the major design decisions are resolved — architecture chosen, interfaces specified, edge cases enumerated, trade-offs decided — is less complex than the same scope arriving open-ended. The pre-resolved decisions don't disappear; they're paid for upstream by whoever wrote the brief.
...

> DEVELOPER

actually not what's a tiny megaplan?

> AGENT

A tiny megaplan = `basic/light` — the smallest configuration the harness runs:

- **Tier 1** (`basic`): DeepSeek plans, Kimi critiques, DeepSeek executes. All cheap open-source.
- **`light` robustness**: 5 phases (plan → critique → revise → finalize → execute). Drops prep, gate, and review.
- **Cost**: ~$0.50–2 total.
- **Time**: typically 5–15 minutes wall-clock.

Below that there's only "skip megaplan entirely". So when the question is "is this even worth running megaplan for?", the comparison is: `basic/light` vs. just doing the work directly (or delegating to a subagent).

The natural place a subagent slots in is *between* "skip" and `basic/light` — when the task is too big to hold in your head but doesn't need any harness rigor (no plan, no critique, no review). Examples: "implement this self-contained function", "refactor these 3 callsites", "write tests for this module". A subagent (codex-subagent or general-purpose) does it in one shot with its own context.

So the rubric currently has 5 levels (skip / light / standard / robust / superrobust). Adding subagent guidance would slot in between skip and light — "skip the harness but offload to a subagent" — which is what I think you were going to ask me to write into the rubric. Want me to add that row?

> DEVELOPER

what does light do specifically?

> AGENT

`light` runs 5 phases instead of `standard`'s 8:

**Runs:**
1. **plan** — initial plan generation
2. **critique** — ONE critique pass (vs. 4 at standard, 8 at robust)
3. **revise** — one revision based on the critique
4. **finalize** — consolidate into the execution checklist (the 11-task `final.md`-style doc)
5. **execute** — implement

**Skips:**
- **prep** — no pre-plan research/discovery phase. Planner goes straight from brief to draft plan.
- **gate** — no quality-gate decision between planning and execution. Whatever revise produces goes straight to finalize and execute.
- **review** — no post-execution audit. Once code is written, it's done.

**Implications in practice:**
- Brief ambiguities get carried into the plan (no prep to surface them).
- One critic pass means real issues might slip through (with `standard`'s 4 passes you get genuine back-and-forth).
- No safety net after execute — bugs surface in your tests, not in a review phase.

**Right for:** small/scoped well-known features, mechanical refactors, low blast radius, brief is unambiguous, mistakes cheap to fix.

**Wrong for:** anything cross-cutting, unfamiliar code, ambiguous brief, anything where you'd actually want a second opinion before merging.

So vs. `standard`: you trade about half the cost and half the wall-clock for losing prep (forced research), the gate (forced quality check), and the post-mortem review. Three real safety nets removed.

> AGENT

Found it. `tiny` IS a real robustness level — `ROBUSTNESS_LEVELS = ("tiny", "light", "standard", "robust", "superrobust")` in `types.py:333`. But it's not documented in the rubric, and it's a bit of a misnomer:

**What `tiny` actually does in code:**
- Same 5 phases as `light` (plan → critique → revise → finalize → execute). Its override entry is literally `"tiny": {}` (empty).
- The **one functional difference**: at `tiny`, the harness explicitly disables the "creative-mode shortcut" — when running in `--mode metaplan` (doc mode), `light` would normally bypass critique and jump plan → finalize directly. `tiny` forces the critique+revise pass to still run even in doc mode.

**Net effect:**
- In **code mode**: `tiny` ≡ `light`. No observable phase difference.
- In **doc mode**: `tiny` is *more thorough* than `light` (forces critique to happen).

So despite the name suggesting "smaller than light", `tiny` is functionally either equal to `light` or slightly more rigorous depending on mode. **There's no level smaller than `light`** — below light is just "skip megaplan entirely".

This looks like either:
1. Dead/half-implemented intent (someone meant to make `tiny` skip more, but never did)
2. A specific niche fix for doc mode that got an oversized name

Worth deciding which: either rename to something accurate (`force-critique`?) or actually make `tiny` skip more phases (e.g., skip critique entirely in code mode → just plan → finalize → execute). Want me to dig into which it is?

> AGENT

Found it — `tiny` actually already mostly does what you want. Looking at `handlers/critique.py:56`:

- At `tiny`, the critique phase **short-circuits to a stub** — no LLM critic runs. It writes `critique_v1.json` with empty `checks` and only verifiability flags (mechanical checks of success criteria), then `gate.json` recommends `ITERATE`/`PROCEED` without thinking.
- Review is **fully skipped** (same as light).
- **Real LLM phases at tiny**: just `plan` and `execute`. Finalize runs but it's mechanical (consolidates plan into checklist).

So substantively `tiny` is already "plan + execute, no critic, no reviewer." The critique/finalize artifacts exist only so downstream consumers don't break.

**Two possible cleanups:**

1. **Document only** — the existing implementation is fine, I just add a `tiny` row to the rubric describing what it really is.
2. **Document + simplify the state machine** — strip critique out of the workflow entirely so `tiny` is genuinely just plan → finalize → execute (3 phases, no stub critique artifacts). This would let me delete the `if robustness == "tiny"` branches in `critique.py` and `audits/robustness.py`. More invasive — touches `test_tiny_robustness.py` and a handful of other places.

    if robustness in {"light", "tiny"}:

def creative_checks_for_robustness(form: Any, robustness: str) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]:

{
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_tiny_robustness.py",
  "content": "from __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nimport megaplan\nfrom megaplan._core import load_plan\nfrom megaplan.types import CliError\nfrom tests.test_handle_review_robustness import PlanFixture, _advance_to_executed, _make_plan_fixture\n\n\ndef _artifact_names(plan_dir: Path) -> set[str]:\n    return {path.name for path in plan_dir.iterdir() if path.is_file()}\n\n\ndef _advance_tiny_to_executed(fixture: PlanFixture) -> dict[str, object]:\n    args = fixture.make_args(plan=fixture.plan_name)\n    megaplan.handle_plan(fixture.root, args)\n    megaplan.handle_finalize(fixture.root, args)\n    execute = megaplan.handle_execute(\n        fixture.root,\n        fixture.make_args(plan=fixture.plan_name, confirm_destructive=True, user_approved=True),\n    )\n    _plan_dir, final_state = load_plan(fixture.root, fixture.plan_name)\n    return {\"state\": final_state, \"execute\": execute}\n\n\ndef test_tiny_robustness_skips_critique_and_review(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n) -> None:\n    tiny_fixture = _make_plan_fixture(tmp_path, monkeypatch, robustness=\"tiny\")\n\n    _plan_dir, tiny_initial_state = load_plan(tiny_fixture.root, tiny_fixture.plan_name)\n    # From INITIALIZED at tiny, next valid step is plan (not prep).\n    assert \"plan\" in megaplan.infer_next_steps(tiny_initial_state)\n\n    # Drive the plan through to DONE.\n    result = _advance_tiny_to_executed(tiny_fixture)\n\n    # After plan, the only valid next step is finalize (critique is bypassed).\n    # We can't easily snapshot mid-flight here, but we can assert the terminal\n    # artifact set: no critique_v*.json files were produced.\n    tiny_artifacts = _artifact_names(tiny_fixture.plan_dir)\n    assert not any(name.startswith(\"critique_v\") for name in tiny_artifacts), (\n        f\"tiny should not produce critique artifacts, got: {sorted(tiny_artifacts)}\"\n    )\n    assert \"review.json\" not in tiny_artifacts\n    assert \"plan_v1.md\" in tiny_artifacts\n    assert \"finalize.json\" in tiny_artifacts\n    assert \"state.json\" in tiny_artifacts\n\n    # Final state is DONE; no review fired.\n    assert result[\"execute\"][\"state\"] == megaplan.STATE_DONE\n    assert result[\"state\"][\"current_state\"] == megaplan.STATE_DONE\n    assert \"review\" not in megaplan.infer_next_steps(\n        {\"current_state\": megaplan.STATE_EXECUTED, \"last_gate\": {}, \"config\": {\"robustness\": \"tiny\"}}\n    )\n\n\ndef test_tiny_robustness_rejects_manual_critique(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Calling `megaplan critique` at tiny should error — the phase is skipped.\"\"\"\n    fixture = _make_plan_fixture(tmp_path, monkeypatch, robustness=\"tiny\")\n    megaplan.handle_plan(fixture.root, fixture.make_args(plan=fixture.plan_name))\n\n    with pytest.raises(CliError, match=\"tiny robustness skips critique\"):\n        megaplan.handle_critique(fixture.root, fixture.make_args(plan=fixture.plan_name))\n\n\ndef test_tiny_parity_with_light_terminates_in_done(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Both tiny and light end in STATE_DONE after execute (no review phase).\"\"\"\n    tiny_fixture = _make_plan_fixture(tmp_path, monkeypatch, robustness=\"tiny\")\n    light_fixture = _make_plan_fixture(tmp_path, monkeypatch, robustness=\"light\")\n    standard_fixture = _make_plan_fixture(tmp_path, monkeypatch, robustness=\"standard\")\n\n    _advance_tiny_to_executed(tiny_fixture)\n    _advance_to_executed(light_fixture)\n    _advance_to_executed(standard_fixture)\n\n    _plan_dir, tiny_final = load_plan(tiny_fixture.root, tiny_fixture.plan_name)\n    _plan_dir, light_final = load_plan(light_fixture.root, light_fixture.plan_name)\n    _plan_dir, standard_final = load_plan(standard_fixture.root, standard_fixture.plan_name)\n\n    assert tiny_final[\"current_state\"] == megaplan.STATE_DONE\n    assert light_final[\"current_state\"] == megaplan.STATE_DONE\n    # standard halts at EXECUTED awaiting review (review fires separately).\n    assert standard_final[\"current_state\"] == megaplan.STATE_EXECUTED\n"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/data/rubric_skill.md",
  "old_string": "| Setting | Workflow | When to use |\n|---|---|---|\n| (skip megaplan) | — | Single-file fix, anything you can hold in your head. Just do the work directly. |\n| `light` | plan → critique → revise → finalize → execute (no prep, no gate, no review) | Small/scoped, well-known feature, low blast radius. ~5 phases instead of 8. |\n| `standard` *(default)* | prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review; 4 critique checks | Cross-cutting, unfamiliar code, ambiguous brief. **Fine for almost everything.** |\n| `robust` | Same shape as `standard`, 8 critique checks + parallel critique | Security, data migration, public API contract — anything where a regression = production incident. **Extremely rare.** You should be able to name the specific stakes that warrant it. |\n| `superrobust` | `robust` + parallel review | Both deep critique *and* concurrent review matter. **Vanishingly rare.** Only when the user specifically asks for it. |\n\n`standard` is home base. `light` is a cost optimization for cleanly-scoped work. `robust` should feel exceptional — you're saying \"this regression would page someone.\" `superrobust` is a user-requested override, never a default.",
  "new_string": "| Setting | Workflow | When to use |\n|---|---|---|\n| (skip megaplan) | — | Single-file fix, anything you can hold in your head. Just do the work directly — or delegate to a subagent if the work is too big to hold in context but doesn't need any harness rigor. |\n| `tiny` | plan → finalize → execute (no prep, no critique, no gate, no review) | Crisp, well-scoped task where you mostly just want the planner to lay out the steps before the executor runs. No second opinion, no post-mortem. 3 phases. **Strictly cheaper than `light`.** Reach for it when the brief is unambiguous and the work is mechanical enough that critique would be a no-op. |\n| `light` | plan → critique → revise → finalize → execute (no prep, no gate, no review) | Small/scoped, well-known feature, low blast radius — but you want **one** sense-check pass on the plan before committing. ~5 phases instead of 8. |\n| `standard` *(default)* | prep → plan → critique → gate → revise → finalize → execute → review; 4 critique checks | Cross-cutting, unfamiliar code, ambiguous brief. **Fine for almost everything.** |\n| `robust` | Same shape as `standard`, 8 critique checks + parallel critique | Security, data migration, public API contract — anything where a regression = production incident. **Extremely rare.** You should be able to name the specific stakes that warrant it. |\n| `superrobust` | `robust` + parallel review | Both deep critique *and* concurrent review matter. **Vanishingly rare.** Only when the user specifically asks for it. |\n\n`standard` is home base. `light` is a cost optimization for cleanly-scoped work that still wants a sense-check. `tiny` is for when even one critique pass is overkill — you're paying for the planner-then-executor split and nothing else. `robust` should feel exceptional — you're saying \"this regression would page someone.\" `superrobust` is a user-requested override, never a default.\n\n**Where `tiny` slots in:** between \"skip megaplan\" and `light`. Skip megaplan when you can do it inline or hand it to a subagent. Pick `tiny` when the work is multi-step enough to deserve a written plan + an executor pass, but the plan doesn't need a critic. Pick `light` when you want one critique pass on the plan before execution. The hops are real — don't reach past `tiny` reflexively."
}
```

> AGENT

Pushed as `0a7da805`. Net: **+110 / −142 across 8 files** (net negative — code got smaller).

Summary of what changed:

**Workflow (`_core/workflow.py`):** `tiny` now has a real override that routes `STATE_PLANNED → finalize → STATE_GATED`, genuinely bypassing critique. Removed the `creative=tiny` flag-suppression hack — no longer needed.

**Handlers:**
- `critique.py`: replaced the 65-line stub-critique branch with a `CliError` that tells the user critique is skipped at tiny (-67 lines net there)
- `finalize.py`: 3-line change to allow `STATE_PLANNED → finalize` at tiny
- `execute.py`: short-circuits to `STATE_DONE` at tiny without writing stub `review.json` (light keeps its stub for backwards compat — only tiny was cleaned)

**Rubric:** `tiny` documented as its own row between "skip megaplan" and `light`. Workflow line reads `plan → finalize → execute (no prep, no critique, no gate, no review)`.

**Tests:** updated to assert the new artifact set (no `critique_v*.json`, no `review.json` at tiny), and added `test_tiny_robustness_rejects_manual_critique` to lock in the new error path. 1424 tests pass.

The auto-sync already refreshed the installed `megaplan-rubric` skill, so the next time anything reads `/megaplan-rubric` it'll see the new `tiny` row.

> AGENT

Yes — partially. Megaplan writes a `step_receipt_<phase>_v<iter>.json` per phase per attempt, and they capture: `cost_usd`, `duration_ms`, `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `agent`, `model_configured`, `model_actual`, `session_id`, prompt hashes, timestamps. But the data quality is uneven — here's the actual tickets-mvp run:

```
step           agent    model                     cost   prompt_tok  compl_tok    min
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
plan_v1        claude   claude:low           $  0.9072            0          0    3.2
critique_v1    claude   claude:low           $  2.1475            0          0    4.0
critique_v2    claude   claude:low           $  1.2504            0          0    1.7
critique_v3    claude   claude:low           $  1.5097            0          0    1.6
critique_v4    claude   claude:low           $  0.8359            0          0    0.9
revise_v2      claude   claude:low           $  2.6371            0          0   12.4
revise_v3      claude   claude:low           $  1.2895            0          0    4.9
revise_v4      claude   claude:low           $  1.2689            0          0    5.4
gate_v1        hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000       44,624      2,172    1.7
gate_v2        hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000       52,542      2,291    1.2
gate_v3        hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000       53,410      3,912    2.6
gate_v4        hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000      101,034      7,100    5.0
finalize_v4    hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000      787,185     12,608    7.7
execute_v4     hermes   deepseek-v4-pro      $  0.0000            0          0   10.8
review_v4      claude   claude:low           $  1.0077            0          0    0.7
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TOTAL                                        $ 12.8539    1,038,795     28,083   63.7
```

**The visible gaps:**

1. **Claude phases: cost ✅, tokens ❌.** Claude's billing API returns `cost_usd` but megaplan isn't reading the token counts out of the Claude CLI response. So `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` are 0 for every Claude phase.
2. **Hermes (Fireworks DeepSeek) phases: tokens ✅, cost ❌.** Fireworks returns token counts, but no per-token pricing is wired up for them, so cost is $0.00 — even though execute ran for 10.8 min on DeepSeek doing real work.
3. **Execute is missing both.** Even the token count is 0 for execute, despite it running 10.8 minutes. Whatever path execute uses to spawn workers, the receipt isn't capturing the worker's token usage.

So the headline `total_cost_usd: 12.85` undercounts the actual spend — it only sums what gets reported, missing the DeepSeek execute consumption. Useful insight: **almost all the visible cost is Claude on critique/revise**. 4 critique+revise cycles ate $11+ alone.

This looks worth fixing. Want me to dig into:

1. **Why Claude phases lose token counts** — likely a parsing bug in how the hermes/claude worker reads the CLI output.
2. **Why execute reports 0 tokens** — different code path, maybe doesn't write through the same receipt.
3. **Wire Fireworks pricing** — straightforward, just need $/Mtok numbers and a lookup table.

Any of these worth taking on now, or file a ticket and move on?

def parse_claude_envelope(raw: str) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]]:
    try:
        envelope = json.loads(raw)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
        raise CliError("parse_error", f"Claude output was not valid JSON: {exc}", extra={"raw_output": raw}) from exc
    if isinstance(envelope, dict) and envelope.get("is_error"):
        message = envelope.get("result") or envelope.get("message") or "Claude returned an error"
        lower = str(message).lower()
        error_code = "worker_error"
        if any(pattern in lower for pattern in ("not logged in", "/login", "unauthorized", "authentication")):
            error_code = "auth_error"
        raise CliError(error_code, f"Claude step failed: {message}", extra={"raw_output": raw})
    # When using --json-schema, structured output lives in "structured_output"
---


def _codex_step_cost(
    session_id: str | None,
    session_entry: dict[str, Any],
) -> tuple[float, int, int, str | None, dict[str, Any] | None]:
    """Compute incremental cost (USD) and token deltas for one codex step.

    Looks up the rollout JSONL for ``session_id``, reads the cumulative
    ``total_token_usage``, and subtracts the ``last_total_tokens`` snapshot
    stored on ``session_entry`` (mutated in place to record the new totals).

    Returns ``(cost_usd, prompt_tokens_delta, completion_tokens_delta,
    model, current_total_usage)``. Any failure to read the JSONL or compute
    the delta returns zeros and a ``None`` usage blob — never raises.
    """
    from megaplan.codex_pricing import cost_from_usage

    if not session_id:
        return 0.0, 0, 0, None, None
    path = _codex_session_jsonl_path(session_id)

def _extract_json_from_reasoning(messages: list) -> dict | None:
    """Extract JSON from the last assistant message's reasoning field.

    Some models (GLM-5) wrap their entire response in think/reasoning tags,
    so the content field is empty but reasoning contains valid JSON.
    """
    for msg in reversed(messages):
        if msg.get("role") != "assistant":
            continue
        for field in ("reasoning", "reasoning_content"):
            text = msg.get(field)

> AGENT

Full picture now. There are three different worker code paths and they each have a different bug shape:

## 1. Claude — **Easy fix.** Bug confirmed.

`workers.py:1845-1858` (`run_claude_step`) reads the Claude CLI's JSON envelope but only pulls `total_cost_usd` and `session_id` out. The envelope **already contains** a `usage` dict with `input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `output_tokens` — Claude's CLI emits all of it. The fields just aren't read.

Fix is ~4 lines:
```python
usage = envelope.get("usage", {}) or {}
prompt_tokens = (
    int(usage.get("input_tokens", 0) or 0)
    + int(usage.get("cache_read_input_tokens", 0) or 0)
    + int(usage.get("cache_creation_input_tokens", 0) or 0)
)
completion_tokens = int(usage.get("output_tokens", 0) or 0)
```
…and pass them to the `WorkerResult(...)` constructor.

## 2. Codex — Already implemented. Probably works.

`workers.py:767` (`_codex_step_cost`) reads the codex rollout JSONL (`info.total_token_usage`), subtracts the previous snapshot stored in the session entry to get the *delta* for this step, and uses `megaplan/codex_pricing.py` for `$/Mtok` lookup → returns `(cost_usd, prompt_delta, completion_delta, model, current)`. Plugs into the WorkerResult at `workers.py:2274-2276`.

So tokens AND cost should both flow. The tickets-mvp run used Claude+hermes, not Codex, so we don't have receipts to verify with. Would need a Codex run to confirm.

## 3. Hermes/Fireworks (DeepSeek, Kimi) — **Tokens work; cost is missing pricing data.**

`hermes_worker.py:622-626` already reads `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens` from the hermes_cli result and they flow through (we saw 787k tokens on finalize_v4). Cost is `result.get("estimated_cost_usd", 0.0)` — hermes_cli returns **0** because no Fireworks pricing is wired up.

Two ways to fix:

- **(a)** Have hermes_cli compute cost before returning it. Touches a vendored subtree (`megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/`).
- **(b)** Mirror the `codex_pricing.py` pattern: add `megaplan/fireworks_pricing.py` with $/Mtok tables for DeepSeek V4 Pro, Kimi K2, etc. Compute cost in megaplan after reading tokens from the hermes result. **Cleaner and matches the existing codex pattern.**

Pricing numbers (rough — would need to verify from Fireworks' published pricing page):
- DeepSeek V4 Pro: ~$0.55 / $2.19 per Mtok (in/out)
- Kimi K2: ~$0.60 / $2.50 per Mtok

## Bonus anomaly worth flagging

`execute_v4` shows **0 tokens** even though it's a hermes phase that ran for 10.8 minutes. That's different from the other gaps: hermes execute spawns per-task child workers (one per task in the batch), and the parent execute receipt isn't aggregating child token usage. So even after fixes (1) and (3b), execute will still under-report unless the per-task children write their own receipts or are summed into the parent.

## Suggested order

1. Fix Claude tokens (4 lines, trivial, biggest visible gap).
2. Add `fireworks_pricing.py` and wire it into hermes worker (small, isolated).
3. Verify Codex with a real run when one happens.
4. File a ticket for the execute aggregation problem — bigger investigation, probably its own sprint.

Want me to do (1) and (2) now, or file tickets for all of them and move on?

<result>All 1425 tests pass, 0 new failures. Summary report:

## Findings

**Root cause:** `megaplan/execute/core.py::dispatch_execute_auto_loop` (the multi-batch path that produces `execution.json` + `step_receipt_execute_v&lt;iter&gt;.json`) constructs its aggregate `receipt_worker` *without* the `prompt_tokens` / `completion_tokens` / `total_tokens` fields. They default to 0 in `WorkerResult`. Per-batch `result.worker.prompt_tokens` *is* populated correctly by `hermes_worker.py:624–635` — the loop just dropped them on the floor while it was summing `total_duration_ms` and `total_cost_usd`.

The single-batch path (`dispatch_execute_one_batch`, lines 887–897) already forwarded these fields, so the bug only manifested for multi-batch executes.

## Changes

**`megaplan/execute/core.py`** (3 small edits, ~10 LOC, no behavior change beyond the bug fix):
- Lines 1207–1211: added `total_prompt_tokens` / `total_completion_tokens` / `total_total_tokens` accumulators alongside `total_duration_ms` / `total_cost_usd`.
- Lines 1289–1294: increment them per batch from `result.worker.*` in the same place duration/cost are summed.
- Lines 1473–1483: forward them into the aggregate `WorkerResult(...)`.

**`tests/test_execute.py`** (~35 LOC new test, `test_auto_loop_aggregates_worker_tokens_into_receipt`): wraps `_hermes_style_worker` to emit non-zero tokens and asserts `step_receipt_execute_v1.json` records them. Verified the test correctly fails on unpatched `main` (`0 == 12345`) and passes after the fix.

**`docs/execute-token-aggregation.md`** (new): root cause writeup, file/line references, risk analysis, and an "out of scope" list (`cost_usd=0` for fireworks/deepseek pricing; `model_actual`/`rendered_prompt` also missing in auto-loop receipt).

## Tests

`1425 passed, 20 skipped, 3 deselected` — same pass/skip counts as on `main` apart from the +1 new test.

## Open work (not tackled, listed in the doc)

1. `cost_usd=0.0` for hermes/deepseek — pricing not wired for fireworks-hosted models in `hermes_worker.py:623` (`result.get("estimated_cost_usd", 0.0)` returns 0). Separate fix.
2. `model_actual: null` and `prompt_hash_*: null` in the auto-loop receipt — same construction site omits `rendered_prompt` and `model_actual`. Cheap follow-up but semantically fuzzier (which batch's prompt do you hash?).

Diagnosis doc: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/docs/execute-token-aggregation.md`. Nothing committed.</result>

## Report

**Changes made:**

1. **`megaplan/workers.py`** — Added helper `_extract_claude_usage(envelope)` at line 835 that sums `input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens` for `prompt_tokens` and uses `output_tokens` for `completion_tokens`, with defensive `_safe_int` handling. Wired into `run_claude_step`'s `WorkerResult(...)` call (now line 1893–1905), passing `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, and their sum as `total_tokens`.

2. **`megaplan/fireworks_pricing.py`** — New file mirroring `codex_pricing.py` shape. Exports `FIREWORKS_PRICING` table and `cost_from_usage(prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, model)`. Matches on the trailing path segment so full Fireworks model paths resolve correctly.

3. **`megaplan/hermes_worker.py`** (~line 627) — After reading `estimated_cost_usd`/tokens from hermes_cli, if `cost_usd == 0.0` AND we have non-zero tokens AND a `model` is present, fall back to `fireworks_pricing.cost_from_usage(...)`. Never overrides a non-zero hermes-reported cost.

4. **`tests/test_workers.py`** — Two new unit tests for `_extract_claude_usage` (happy path + missing/invalid envelope handling); extended `test_run_claude_step_parses_structured_output` to assert token counts flow into `WorkerResult`.

5. **`tests/test_fireworks_pricing.py`** — New file. 7 tests: known model, full Fireworks path, unknown model returns 0.0, missing/empty model returns 0.0, zero tokens returns 0.0, kimi-k2p6 known, defensive None tokens.

**Test results:** 1434 passed, 20 skipped, 3 deselected (the known-failing-on-main ones). Targeted run also clean.

**Drift from spec — pricing values are UNVERIFIED:**
- I could not find authoritative Fireworks-specific pricing anywhere in the codebase. I did find `megaplan/agent/agent/usage_pricing.py` which has DeepSeek direct-API rates: `deepseek-reasoner` at `$0.55/$2.19` (matches your guess for v4-pro by coincidence), and `deepseek-v4-pro` at `$0.435/$0.87` (a discounted snapshot through 2026-05-31). These are DeepSeek direct, not Fireworks-hosted, so they don't apply.
- No `kimi-k2p6` or moonshot entries anywhere.
- Per the spec instructions, I kept your supplied values (`deepseek-v4-pro: (0.55, 2.19)`, `kimi-k2p6: (0.60, 2.50)`) and added a prominent module-level docstring + inline comment marking them UNVERIFIED. **Please confirm against https://fireworks.ai/pricing before relying on these for billing-grade accounting.**

No commits made.</result>

1	"""Fireworks AI pricing table (USD per million tokens).
2	
3	Sourced from https://fireworks.ai/pricing (verify on update).
4	
5	NOTE: The pricing values below are user-supplied rough estimates and have NOT
6	been verified against Fireworks' published rate card at the time this module
7	was added. No authoritative Fireworks-specific pricing was found elsewhere in
8	the megaplan codebase (``agent/agent/usage_pricing.py`` only carries DeepSeek's
9	direct-API rates, which differ from Fireworks-hosted rates). Please confirm
10	against https://fireworks.ai/pricing and update before relying on these
11	figures for billing-grade accounting.
12	"""
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	from typing import Any  # noqa: F401  (kept for parity with codex_pricing.py)
17	
18	# {model_name_or_prefix: (input_per_mtok_usd, output_per_mtok_usd)}
19	# Match by the *trailing* segment of model_actual: e.g.
20	# "accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro" -> "deepseek-v4-pro".
21	FIREWORKS_PRICING: dict[str, tuple[float, float]] = {
22	    # UNVERIFIED — user-supplied estimates, confirm at fireworks.ai/pricing.
23	    "deepseek-v4-pro": (0.55, 2.19),
24	    "kimi-k2p6": (0.60, 2.50),
25	    # Add others as needed.
26	}
27	
28	DEFAULT_PRICING: tuple[float, float] = (0.0, 0.0)
29	
30	
31	def cost_from_usage(
32	    prompt_tokens: int,
33	    completion_tokens: int,
34	    model: str | None,
35	) -> float:
36	    """Compute USD cost from a token count + model name.
37	
38	    ``model`` can be a full Fireworks path like
39	    ``accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro`` or just the trailing
40	    segment. Unknown models return ``0.0`` (caller should log/flag).
41	    Zero tokens always return ``0.0``.
42	    """
43	    if not model:
44	        return 0.0
45	    short = model.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
46	    rates = FIREWORKS_PRICING.get(short, DEFAULT_PRICING)
47	    try:
48	        prompt = int(prompt_tokens or 0)
49	        completion = int(completion_tokens or 0)
50	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
51	        return 0.0
52	    return (prompt * rates[0] + completion * rates[1]) / 1_000_000
53

If\nhermes starts surfacing a `cached_input_tokens` field, switch to billing\nthose separately at the cached rate.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any  # noqa: F401  (kept for parity with codex_pricing.py)\n\n# {model_name_or_prefix: (input_per_mtok_usd, cached_input_per_mtok_usd, output_per_mtok_usd)}\n# Match by the *trailing* segment of model_actual: e.g.\n# \"accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro\" -> \"deepseek-v4-pro\".\nFIREWORKS_PRICING: dict[str, tuple[float, float, float]] = {\n    \"deepseek-v4-pro\": (1.74, 0.145, 3.48),\n    \"kimi-k2p6\": (0.95, 0.16, 4.00),\n    # Add others as needed.\n}\n\nDEFAULT_PRICING: tuple[float, float, float] = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)\n\n\ndef cost_from_usage(\n    prompt_tokens: int,\n    completion_tokens: int,\n    model: str | None,\n    *,\n    cached_prompt_tokens: int = 0,\n) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Compute USD cost from a token count + model name.\n\n    ``model`` can be a full Fireworks path like\n    ``accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro`` or just the trailing\n    segment. Unknown models return ``0.0`` (caller should log/flag).\n    Zero tokens always return ``0.0``.\n\n    ``cached_prompt_tokens`` is optional — if the caller has a breakdown\n    of cached vs. uncached input, pass the cached count here and the\n    cheaper rate is applied to it. If unset (default), everything in\n    ``prompt_tokens`` is billed at the full input rate.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not model:\n        return 0.0\n    short = model.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1]\n    rates = FIREWORKS_PRICING.get(short, DEFAULT_PRICING)\n    in_rate, cached_rate, out_rate = rates\n    try:\n        prompt = int(prompt_tokens or 0)\n        completion = int(completion_tokens or 0)\n        cached = int(cached_prompt_tokens or 0)\n    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n        return 0.0\n    cached = max(0, min(cached, prompt))  # cached can't exceed prompt total\n    uncached = prompt - cached\n    return (uncached * in_rate + cached * cached_rate + completion * out_rate) / 1_000_000\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Verified Kimi too — both models now have authoritative numbers in the table:

| Model | Input | Cached input | Output |
|---|---|---|---|
| deepseek-v4-pro | $1.74 | $0.145 | $3.48 |
| **kimi-k2p6** | **$0.95** | **$0.16** | **$4.00** |

Kimi's output rate ($4.00/Mtok) is notably more expensive than DeepSeek's ($3.48), even though its input is cheaper. So Kimi-heavy critique passes will cost more per response than I had guessed.

My original guess for Kimi was $0.60 / $2.50 — actual is $0.95 / $4.00. So I'd have under-counted Kimi cost by ~60% across the board.

Final test state: **1,436 passing, 20 skipped, 3 pre-existing deselected.** Ready to commit. Want me to push?

{
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/scripts/backfill_step_receipts.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Backfill missing tokens and cost on historical megaplan step_receipt_*.json files.\n\nThree sources of historical gaps this addresses:\n\n1. **Claude tokens** — Receipts where ``agent == \"claude\"`` and ``cost_usd > 0``\n   but ``prompt_tokens == 0 / completion_tokens == 0``. The original CLI envelope\n   carried the ``usage`` dict; we never read it. Cannot be recovered after the\n   fact unless the raw envelope was stored — we flag these but do not invent\n   numbers.\n\n2. **Fireworks-hosted hermes cost** — Receipts where ``agent == \"hermes\"``,\n   ``prompt_tokens + completion_tokens > 0``, ``cost_usd == 0``, and\n   ``model_actual`` (or ``model_configured``) is a known Fireworks model.\n   ``fireworks_pricing.cost_from_usage`` is authoritative here; we compute and\n   write back additively.\n\n3. **Execute multi-batch token aggregation** — Receipts for ``phase == \"execute\"``\n   where ``prompt_tokens == 0`` but sibling ``execution_batch_*.json`` files in\n   the same plan dir carry per-batch usage. We sum them into the parent receipt.\n\n## Output\n\nBy default the script is **dry-run** — it prints what it would change. Pass\n``--apply`` to actually write. Enriched values are added as new fields\n``cost_usd_backfilled``, ``prompt_tokens_backfilled``, ``completion_tokens_backfilled``\nplus ``backfilled_at`` and ``backfill_source``. The original ``cost_usd`` and\n``*_tokens`` fields are NEVER overwritten — readers that prefer the corrected\nvalues should check ``*_backfilled`` first and fall back to the original.\n\n## Usage\n\n```\n# Dry-run (default) — survey the whole machine, summarize what would change\n./scripts/backfill_step_receipts.py\n\n# Restrict to a specific root\n./scripts/backfill_step_receipts.py --root ~/Documents/megaplan\n\n# Apply the changes\n./scripts/backfill_step_receipts.py --apply\n\n# Verbose — print every receipt that would change\n./scripts/backfill_step_receipts.py --verbose\n```\n\n## Safety\n\n- Never overwrites existing non-zero values.\n- Adds additive fields only.\n- Each write records ``backfilled_at`` (UTC ISO timestamp) and\n  ``backfill_source`` so the provenance is traceable.\n- Idempotent — re-running over already-backfilled receipts is a no-op.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\n# Add repo root to sys.path so we can import the pricing module without installing.\nREPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nsys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT))\n\nfrom megaplan.fireworks_pricing import FIREWORKS_PRICING, cost_from_usage  # noqa: E402\n\n\nDEFAULT_SCAN_ROOTS = [\n    Path.home() / \"Documents\",\n    Path.home() / \"code\",\n    Path.home() / \"projects\",\n    Path.home() / \".megaplan\",  # global plans\n]\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Summary:\n    receipts_scanned: int = 0\n    plans_seen: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)\n    hermes_cost_filled: int = 0\n    hermes_cost_total_usd: float = 0.0\n    execute_tokens_filled: int = 0\n    execute_tokens_total: int = 0\n    claude_tokens_unrecoverable: int = 0\n    already_backfilled: int = 0\n    skipped_unknown_model: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=lambda: defaultdict(int))\n    errors: list[tuple[Path, str]] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n\ndef _now_iso() -> str:\n    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat().replace(\"+00:00\", \"Z\")\n\n\ndef _model_for_receipt(r: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:\n    return r.get(\"model_actual\") or r.get(\"model_configured\")\n\n\ndef _short_model(model: str | None) -> str | None:\n    if not model:\n        return None\n    return model.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1]\n\n\ndef _try_aggregate_batches(plan_dir: Path) -> tuple[int, int] | None:\n    \"\"\"Sum prompt/completion tokens across execution_batch_*.json siblings.\n\n    Returns (prompt, completion) or None if no batch files exist or summed to 0.\n    \"\"\"\n    total_prompt = 0\n    total_completion = 0\n    found_any = False\n    for batch_path in sorted(plan_dir.glob(\"execution_batch_*.json\")):\n        try:\n            data = json.loads(batch_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):\n            continue\n        # Batch artifacts vary in shape — try a few likely keys.\n        for key_set in (\n            (\"prompt_tokens\", \"completion_tokens\"),\n            (\"input_tokens\", \"output_tokens\"),\n        ):\n            p_key, c_key = key_set\n            if p_key in data and c_key in data:\n                total_prompt += int(data.get(p_key) or 0)\n                total_completion += int(data.get(c_key) or 0)\n                found_any = True\n                break\n        # Some batches nest under \"worker\" or \"usage\".\n        for nest_key in (\"worker\", \"usage\"):\n            nested = data.get(nest_key)\n            if isinstance(nested, dict):\n                total_prompt += int(nested.get(\"prompt_tokens\", 0) or 0)\n                total_completion += int(nested.get(\"completion_tokens\", 0) or 0)\n                if nested.get(\"prompt_tokens\") or nested.get(\"completion_tokens\"):\n                    found_any = True\n    if not found_any or (total_prompt == 0 and total_completion == 0):\n        return None\n    return total_prompt, total_completion\n\n\ndef _enrich_receipt(receipt_path: Path, dry_run: bool, summary: Summary, verbose: bool) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Returns True if a change was made (or would be in dry-run).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        receipt = json.loads(receipt_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:\n        summary.errors.append((receipt_path, str(exc)))\n        return False\n\n    summary.receipts_scanned += 1\n    plan_id = receipt.get(\"plan_id\") or receipt_path.parent.name\n    summary.plans_seen.add(plan_id)\n    plan_dir = receipt_path.parent\n\n    # Skip if already backfilled by a previous run.\n    if \"backfilled_at\" in receipt:\n        summary.already_backfilled += 1\n        return False\n\n    agent = (receipt.get(\"agent\") or \"\").lower()\n    phase = receipt.get(\"phase\") or \"\"\n    cost = float(receipt.get(\"cost_usd\") or 0.0)\n    p_tok = int(receipt.get(\"prompt_tokens\") or 0)\n    c_tok = int(receipt.get(\"completion_tokens\") or 0)\n    model = _model_for_receipt(receipt)\n\n    updates: dict[str, Any] = {}\n    sources: list[str] = []\n\n    # --- 1. Hermes/Fireworks cost backfill ---\n    if agent == \"hermes\" and cost == 0.0 and (p_tok > 0 or c_tok > 0):\n        short = _short_model(model)\n        if short and short in FIREWORKS_PRICING:\n            computed = cost_from_usage(p_tok, c_tok, model)\n            if computed > 0:\n                updates[\"cost_usd_backfilled\"] = round(computed, 6)\n                sources.append(f\"fireworks_pricing:{short}\")\n                summary.hermes_cost_filled += 1\n                summary.hermes_cost_total_usd += computed\n        else:\n            summary.skipped_unknown_model[short or \"<no-model>\"] += 1\n\n    # --- 2. Execute multi-batch token aggregation ---\n    if phase == \"execute\" and p_tok == 0 and c_tok == 0:\n        aggregated = _try_aggregate_batches(plan_dir)\n        if aggregated is not None:\n            agg_p, agg_c = aggregated\n            updates[\"prompt_tokens_backfilled\"] = agg_p\n            updates[\"completion_tokens_backfilled\"] = agg_c\n            updates[\"total_tokens_backfilled\"] = agg_p + agg_c\n            sources.append(\"execution_batches\")\n            summary.execute_tokens_filled += 1\n            summary.execute_tokens_total += agg_p + agg_c\n            # If we now have hermes tokens for an execute receipt, also try cost.\n            if agent == \"hermes\" and \"cost_usd_backfilled\" not in updates:\n                short = _short_model(model)\n                if short and short in FIREWORKS_PRICING:\n                    computed = cost_from_usage(agg_p, agg_c, model)\n                    if computed > 0:\n                        updates[\"cost_usd_backfilled\"] = round(computed, 6)\n                        if \"fireworks_pricing\" not in \",\".join(sources):\n                            sources.append(f\"fireworks_pricing:{short}\")\n                        summary.hermes_cost_filled += 1\n                        summary.hermes_cost_total_usd += computed\n\n    # --- 3. Claude tokens — flag, do not invent ---\n    if agent == \"claude\" and cost > 0.0 and p_tok == 0 and c_tok == 0:\n        summary.claude_tokens_unrecoverable += 1\n\n    if not updates:\n        return False\n\n    updates[\"backfilled_at\"] = _now_iso()\n    updates[\"backfill_source\"] = \",\".join(sources)\n\n    if verbose:\n        print(f\"  {receipt_path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT.parent) if REPO_ROOT.parent in receipt_path.parents else receipt_path}\")\n        for k, v in updates.items():\n            print(f\"    {k}: {v}\")\n\n    if not dry_run:\n        receipt.update(updates)\n        tmp = receipt_path.with_suffix(receipt_path.suffix + \".tmp\")\n        tmp.write_text(json.dumps(receipt, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        os.replace(tmp, receipt_path)\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef _find_receipts(roots: list[Path]) -> list[Path]:\n    seen: set[Path] = set()\n    receipts: list[Path] = []\n    for root in roots:\n        if not root.exists():\n            continue\n        for path in root.rglob(\"step_receipt_*.json\"):\n            try:\n                resolved = path.resolve()\n            except OSError:\n                continue\n            if resolved in seen:\n                continue\n            seen.add(resolved)\n            receipts.append(path)\n    return receipts\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=__doc__,\n        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--apply\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Write changes. Default is dry-run.\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--root\",\n        action=\"append\",\n        default=None,\n        type=Path,\n        help=f\"Scan root. Repeatable. Defaults: {[str(r) for r in DEFAULT_SCAN_ROOTS]}\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--verbose\",\n        \"-v\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Print every receipt that gets a change.\",\n    )\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    roots = args.root or DEFAULT_SCAN_ROOTS\n    print(f\"Scanning roots: {[str(r) for r in roots]}\")\n    receipts = _find_receipts(roots)\n    print(f\"Found {len(receipts)} step_receipt_*.json files\\n\")\n\n    summary = Summary()\n    changed = 0\n    for receipt_path in receipts:\n        if _enrich_receipt(receipt_path, dry_run=not args.apply, summary=summary, verbose=args.verbose):\n            changed += 1\n\n    print()\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(f\"{'DRY RUN — no files written' if not args.apply else 'APPLIED — files updated'}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(f\"Receipts scanned:               {summary.receipts_scanned}\")\n    print(f\"Unique plans seen:              {len(summary.plans_seen)}\")\n    print(f\"Receipts {'changed' if args.apply else 'that would change'}: {changed}\")\n    print(f\"Receipts already backfilled:    {summary.already_backfilled}\")\n    print()\n    print(\"Hermes/Fireworks cost backfill:\")\n    print(f\"  receipts:                     {summary.hermes_cost_filled}\")\n    print(f\"  total cost recovered:         ${summary.hermes_cost_total_usd:,.2f}\")\n    print()\n    print(\"Execute multi-batch tokens:\")\n    print(f\"  receipts:                     {summary.execute_tokens_filled}\")\n    print(f\"  total tokens recovered:       {summary.execute_tokens_total:,}\")\n    print()\n    print(f\"Claude receipts missing tokens (unrecoverable): {summary.claude_tokens_unrecoverable}\")\n    if summary.skipped_unknown_model:\n        print()\n        print(\"Hermes receipts skipped — model not in FIREWORKS_PRICING:\")\n        for short, count in sorted(summary.skipped_unknown_model.items(), key=lambda kv: -kv[1]):\n            print(f\"  {short!r}: {count}\")\n    if summary.errors:\n        print()\n        print(f\"Errors reading {len(summary.errors)} receipts:\")\n        for path, err in summary.errors[:10]:\n            print(f\"  {path}: {err}\")\n        if len(summary.errors) > 10:\n            print(f\"  ... and {len(summary.errors) - 10} more\")\n\n    if not args.apply and changed > 0:\n        print()\n        print(f\"Re-run with --apply to write {changed} change(s).\")\n\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}

Default ratio is 10:1, which is a\nreasonable middle ground for megaplan-style usage (long contextual prompts,\nmodest structured outputs). Override per-call if you have a better prior.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\n# {model_match: (input, cached_read, cached_write, output)} per 1M tokens, USD.\n#\n# Match is by *prefix* on the trailing path segment, so:\n#   \"claude-opus-4-20250514\"       -> matches \"claude-opus-4\"\n#   \"anthropic/claude-opus-4-7\"    -> matches \"claude-opus-4\"\n#   \"claude-opus-4.6\"              -> matches \"claude-opus-4\"\n# Use the longest prefix that still distinguishes the model family.\nCLAUDE_PRICING: dict[str, tuple[float, float, float, float]] = {\n    \"claude-opus-4\": (15.00, 1.50, 18.75, 75.00),\n    \"claude-sonnet-4\": (3.00, 0.30, 3.75, 15.00),\n    \"claude-haiku-4\": (0.25, 0.025, 0.3125, 1.25),\n    # Older / fallback families.\n    \"claude-3-opus\": (15.00, 1.50, 18.75, 75.00),\n    \"claude-3-5-sonnet\": (3.00, 0.30, 3.75, 15.00),\n    \"claude-3-5-haiku\": (0.80, 0.08, 1.00, 4.00),\n}\n\nDEFAULT_MODEL_FAMILY = \"claude-opus-4\"\n\"\"\"Family to assume when model_actual is missing or generic.\n\nMegaplan's default ``claude`` profile slot resolves to Opus, so historical\nreceipts with ``model_actual=None`` or ``model_configured='claude'`` are\noverwhelmingly Opus runs.\n\"\"\"\n\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_COMPLETION_RATIO = 10.0\n\"\"\"Default assumption for reverse estimation: prompts are 10× completions.\n\nCalibrated against live megaplan receipts where input:output runs ~10-60:1\nfor plan/critique phases. 10:1 is conservative (under-estimates total tokens\nfor very prompt-heavy runs).\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _resolve_rates(model: str | None) -> tuple[float, float, float, float] | None:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(input, cached_read, cached_write, output)`` for a model, or None.\"\"\"\n    if not model:\n        return None\n    short = model.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1].lower()\n    # Strip date suffix like \"-20250514\" or version suffix like \"-7\" so the\n    # prefix match handles \"claude-opus-4-7\" / \"claude-opus-4.6\" / etc.\n    for prefix, rates in CLAUDE_PRICING.items():\n        if short.startswith(prefix):\n            return rates\n    return None\n\n\ndef cost_from_usage(\n    prompt_tokens: int,\n    completion_tokens: int,\n    model: str | None,\n    *,\n    cached_read_tokens: int = 0,\n    cached_write_tokens: int = 0,\n) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Compute USD cost from a token count + model name.\n\n    ``cached_read_tokens`` and ``cached_write_tokens`` are optional. If unset,\n    everything in ``prompt_tokens`` is billed at the full input rate.\n    Otherwise the cached counts are billed at the cheaper rates and only the\n    remainder of ``prompt_tokens`` is billed at the full input rate.\n    \"\"\"\n    rates = _resolve_rates(model)\n    if rates is None:\n        return 0.0\n    in_rate, cr_rate, cw_rate, out_rate = rates\n    try:\n        p = int(prompt_tokens or 0)\n        c = int(completion_tokens or 0)\n        cr = int(cached_read_tokens or 0)\n        cw = int(cached_write_tokens or 0)\n    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n        return 0.0\n    cr = max(0, min(cr, p))\n    cw = max(0, min(cw, p - cr))\n    uncached = max(0, p - cr - cw)\n    return (uncached * in_rate + cr * cr_rate + cw * cw_rate + c * out_rate) / 1_000_000\n\n\ndef estimate_tokens_from_cost(\n    cost_usd: float,\n    model: str | None = None,\n    *,\n    ratio: float = DEFAULT_PROMPT_COMPLETION_RATIO,\n) -> tuple[int, int] | None:\n    \"\"\"Back out approximate ``(prompt_tokens, completion_tokens)`` from a cost.\n\n    Lossy reverse of ``cost_from_usage`` — assumes ``prompt = ratio *\n    completion`` and that all input is uncached (worst case for the\n    estimate, since uncached input is the most expensive input rate).\n\n    Returns ``None`` if the model can't be resolved or the cost is zero.\n\n    Example: $0.225 of Opus 4 at the default 10:1 ratio yields roughly\n    1000 completion + 10000 prompt = 11000 tokens estimated.\n    \"\"\"\n    if cost_usd <= 0:\n        return None\n    rates = _resolve_rates(model or DEFAULT_MODEL_FAMILY)\n    if rates is None:\n        return None\n    in_rate, _cr, _cw, out_rate = rates\n    # cost = (P * in_rate + C * out_rate) / 1M, with P = ratio * C\n    # => C = cost * 1M / (ratio * in_rate + out_rate)\n    denominator = ratio * in_rate + out_rate\n    if denominator <= 0:\n        return None\n    completion = (cost_usd * 1_000_000) / denominator\n    prompt = ratio * completion\n    return int(round(prompt)), int(round(completion))\n"
}

1M prompt + 1M completion at Opus rates = $15 + $75 = $90\n    assert cost_from_usage(1_000_000, 1_000_000, \"claude-opus-4-7\") == pytest.approx(90.0)\n\n\ndef test_sonnet_4_cost() -> None:\n    # 1M prompt + 1M completion at Sonnet 4 rates = $3 + $15 = $18\n    assert cost_from_usage(1_000_000, 1_000_000, \"claude-sonnet-4-6\") == pytest.approx(18.0)\n\n\ndef test_opus_4_with_dated_suffix() -> None:\n    # Prefix match should handle Anthropic's dated model ids.\n    assert cost_from_usage(\n        1_000_000, 0, \"claude-opus-4-20250514\"\n    ) == pytest.approx(15.0)\n\n\ndef test_full_path_prefix() -> None:\n    short = cost_from_usage(500_000, 100_000, \"claude-opus-4-7\")\n    full = cost_from_usage(500_000, 100_000, \"anthropic/claude-opus-4-7\")\n    assert short == full\n    assert short > 0\n\n\ndef test_unknown_model_returns_zero() -> None:\n    assert cost_from_usage(1_000_000, 1_000_000, \"made-up-model\") == 0.0\n\n\ndef test_none_model_returns_zero() -> None:\n    assert cost_from_usage(1_000_000, 1_000_000, None) == 0.0\n\n\ndef test_cached_tokens_billed_cheaper() -> None:\n    # 1M prompt of which 800k cached_read, 0 completion at Opus.\n    # uncached: 200k * 15 = $3\n    # cached_read: 800k * 1.5 = $1.2\n    # total: $4.20\n    assert cost_from_usage(\n        1_000_000, 0, \"claude-opus-4-7\", cached_read_tokens=800_000\n    ) == pytest.approx(4.20)\n\n\ndef test_estimate_round_trip_default_ratio() -> None:\n    # If we encode tokens at the default 10:1 ratio, compute cost,\n    # then estimate back — we should get the same tokens out.\n    p_in = 10_000\n    c_in = 1_000\n    cost = cost_from_usage(p_in, c_in, \"claude-opus-4-7\")\n    result = estimate_tokens_from_cost(cost, \"claude-opus-4-7\", ratio=10.0)\n    assert result is not None\n    p_out, c_out = result\n    assert p_out == pytest.approx(p_in, abs=1)\n    assert c_out == pytest.approx(c_in, abs=1)\n\n\ndef test_estimate_returns_none_for_zero_cost() -> None:\n    assert estimate_tokens_from_cost(0.0, \"claude-opus-4-7\") is None\n    assert estimate_tokens_from_cost(-1.0, \"claude-opus-4-7\") is None\n\n\ndef test_estimate_returns_none_for_unknown_model() -> None:\n    # Even with a valid cost, can't estimate if model can't be priced.\n    assert estimate_tokens_from_cost(1.0, \"unknown-model\") is None\n\n\ndef test_estimate_falls_back_to_default_family_when_model_none() -> None:\n    # No model given -> defaults to opus (the megaplan default).\n    result = estimate_tokens_from_cost(0.225, None, ratio=10.0)\n    assert result is not None\n    # $0.225 of Opus at 10:1 = ~1000 completion / 10000 prompt\n    p, c = result\n    assert c == pytest.approx(1000, abs=5)\n    assert p == pytest.approx(10000, abs=50)\n\n\ndef test_known_families_have_4_field_rates() -> None:\n    for family, rates in CLAUDE_PRICING.items():\n        assert len(rates) == 4, f\"{family!r} should be (input, cached_read, cached_write, output)\"\n        in_, cr, cw, out = rates\n        assert 0 < cr < in_ < cw < out or 0 < cr < in_ <= cw <= out, (\n            f\"{family!r} unexpected rate ordering: {rates}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef test_default_ratio_is_documented() -> None:\n    assert DEFAULT_PROMPT_COMPLETION_RATIO == 10.0\n"
}
